## 21. What this document supplies, and to which document

**Every feature module reads this section.** Each service is named once here and its rules are in the section named beside it.

**To every feature module — the services, and there is no other route to any of them.**

| Service | What it is | Where |
|---|---|---|
| **the platform file adapter** | every local read and write, its outcome established by re-checking the destination by path after closing, its refusals classified per system, the publication operation, and the folder-identity comparison | [Section 6](#6-the-platform-file-adapter) |
| **the receiving-filesystem collision judgement** | the name check before a transfer and the taken check at publication, with the outcome handed on and no prompt, rename or decision taken | [Section 7](#7-the-receiving-filesystem-collision-judgement) |
| **the downloads folder** | the three per-system routes, proposed and never written into unasked | [Section 8](#8-where-a-download-is-proposed-to-land) |
| **the user home directory reading, and Smoozy's own temporary area** | the system's own call for the user's home directory, never composed, never assembled from the login account name or an environment value, and never [Section 8](#8-where-a-download-is-proposed-to-land)'s Linux route; and one temporary area under the per-user application-data location, protected as the record is, never a directory any module scans for keys, and swept at application start. **Added 2026-08-29, routed in from [Keys](Keys.md#13-unresolved) §13 item 20** | [5.1](#51-one-store-and-its-contents-are-another-documents-list) L3a |
| **the secret adapter and its helper** | put, get, erase, probe, enumerate, with one shared failure classification; a use carried out on a module's behalf, or a handle it can present again, and never a plaintext | [Section 9](#9-the-secret-adapter-and-its-helper) |
| **the name-reading adapter** | the one place the computer name or the login account name is read, one named source per system | [Section 10](#10-the-application-identity-adapter) |
| **the two rate budgets and the session-admission point** | one upload budget, one download budget, in bytes per second, with a non-zero floor per admitted channel and one point that knows every server; **and the achieved-rate reading, one per direction, over the one window of `Server protocol and transfers`' R12a, held rather than aged between publications** (R15a); **and a request's remaining-time estimate, that same window summed over that request's own channels alone, divided into bytes remaining, with the three states carrying no estimate** (R15b) | [Section 11](#11-the-two-rate-budgets-and-the-one-session-admission-point) |
| **the window frame** | given up on all three, the title bar the application's own — the drag region a CSS property on any element, the buttons drawn and wired to the shell's own runtime calls, the height the page's own — with the macOS second route and the per-system residue named | [2.2](#22-the-route-chosen-and-the-properties-it-is-held-to) H6a |
| **the notification service** | the four kinds, the per-system preconditions and permission models, the picture, and the *now* boundary; **and what a per-kind switch may claim — the kind, never the picture and never delivery** (T17a) | [Section 13](#13-notifications) |
| **the suppression table** | one row per module and warning identity, and the one control that clears it | [Section 15](#15-one-control-restores-every-suppressed-warning) |
| **the presentation plumbing** | failure records — **two of them standing at once presented as two, never composed into one statement about a shared cause** (M7a) — the raw record and its four collection outcomes, the disk-full diagnosis, progress, partial and atomic outcomes, and the repairing server's three readings; **and each stream's record presented under the name of the stream it came from, with no line of the ordinary output lifted out of it and nothing composed from it reaching the surface where the reason is said** (M13a, M13b) | [Section 16](#16-progress-and-failure-presentation-plumbing) |
| **the typography application point** | the two substitutions, applied to prose meant for a person and to nothing else | [Section 17](#17-text-rendering-and-the-typography-application-point) |
| **the colour scheme** | one reading — the page's own `prefers-color-scheme` — a change arriving as an event and never on a timer, the `color-scheme` declaration beside every token block, and light where the system has no such setting | [2.6](#26-the-colour-scheme-read-and-declared) |
| **the two faces** | both asked for by generic family and neither shipped, each stack ending in a generic the standard guarantees, and the fixed-width column resting on that guarantee rather than on an OpenType feature | [2.7](#27-the-two-faces) |
| **the verbatim route** | the one way a string that is data reaches a surface — no substitution of any kind, chosen by the module at the moment it hands the string over, and with the typography point the complete set of routes into the interface | [17.3](#173-what-it-must-never-touch) X9 |
| **the menus and the system file picker** | the four menus' integration per platform, with the per-system key mapping of [18.5](#185-the-keyboard-shortcuts-beside-the-menu-items), and one picker service | [Section 18](#18-menus-and-pickers) |
| **the folder-reveal service** | reveal a named file selected in its folder, or open a folder, in the system's own file manager — symbolic links resolved first, the outcome returned to the caller on every system, and *could not be opened* a real outcome rather than silence; platform code beside the shell, which reaches none of it | [18.6](#186-the-folder-reveal-service) |
| **the address-opening service** | open a build-fixed Source code or conditional Donate destination, selected by name, in the user’s own browser; the service says only that it asked | [18.7](#187-the-address-opening-service) K25 |
| **the diary service** | the diary's one writer — entries handed from each module's declared acts (A7) and from [16.8](#168-what-the-plumbing-hands-the-diary-and-the-report-entry-point)'s hooks, written and forced down at the moment each fact becomes true, evicted by whole segments under the fixed cap, with the record's own rules `Reporting a problem` §1, §3 and §4's | [5.3](#53-the-diary-service) |
| **the local record** | the one durable store on this side, built to another document's inventory, with its migrations | [Section 5](#5-the-local-records-database-and-its-migrations) |
| **the updater** | check, fetch, verify, replace, per system, and the one helper Windows needs; the trigger is application start, one repeating check, and **one act of the user's, which starts the same check and never a second mechanism** (Z23, Z26, Z27); the fetch draws from the download budget and is admitted at the desktop point (Z22); **on Linux the verifying key is built into the artifact and is never fetched** (E19) | [Sections 19](#19-build-outputs-signing-and-hosting) and [20](#20-update-verification-and-replacement) |

**To `Server protocol and transfers`.** The **platform file operation** its S18 asks for, chosen: the platform's atomic rename onto the final path, staged on the same filesystem, named per system with Windows unresolved ([6.5](#65-the-publication-operation-and-what-would-demonstrate-it)) — **and what would demonstrate it, with the negative result written down** (F18). The **staged and final objects' stable file identity** its S18 requirement 4 asks for, per system — the `st_dev`/`st_ino` pair on macOS and Linux, the 128-bit file identifier with the volume serial number on Windows — with S15's identity-unavailable branch taken where the call refuses or a volume's own identifiers are documented changeable (F15a). The **one upload budget and one download budget** its R1 leaves here, in bytes per second, with **the floor its C19 needs** ([11.2](#112-how-the-budget-is-divided-which-is-the-constraint-that-was-handed-over)). The **one desktop-side admission point** its T5 requires, admitting transfer channels across every server and the update fetch beside them, never gating the command or reading channels, and guaranteeing every class a share of its own admissible count ([11.3](#113-the-one-desktop-side-admission-point) R11, R17, R18) — **including the update fetch, which its T5a names here with no class and which R19 declares as one, added 2026-08-29**. And the **automatic supervisor-unavailable repair trigger** that sends its §5 request, with the moments it fires at ([Section 12](#12-the-automatic-supervisor-unavailable-repair-trigger)).

**To `Download`.** The collision judgement's outcome — which of the name check's four, whether the name was taken, and, where the system supplies it, the stored name that took it ([7.5](#75-what-is-handed-to-download)). The downloads folder and Apple's prohibition on writing into it unasked ([Section 8](#8-where-a-download-is-proposed-to-land)). The platform file adapter's write outcomes, including that **the count of what arrived is never the count of what was fetched** (C21). **And a fourth refusal class, supplied 2026-08-23 and routed rather than written there: *the write failed at the device*, cause unnamed, for the `EIO` a detached volume gives at `write()`** ([6.1](#61-the-one-rule-the-whole-adapter-is-built-on) F4a). `Download` D1 lists three and consumes them without re-deriving, so the line naming the fourth is that document's to add; the gap it records in its own §15 item 23 is closed on this side. **Its Replace and Keep rules are the user-facing response built on all of that and never a second collision judgement**, and a name it composes is judged again like any other (C20). **And the folder-reveal service ([18.6](#186-the-folder-reveal-service)), supplied 2026-08-25, which is what its H7's *"One place, and it opens"* has been missing since it was written**: the opening is attempted through the service, its outcome comes back, and H8's *a place Smoozy cannot open* is thereby a presentable state rather than a claim with no mechanism under it.

**To `File Browser`.** The system file picker ([18.4](#184-the-system-file-picker)) — **Row 307 is not answered here**. The platform file adapter for anything it writes locally — **and for the local *reads* that document's [§12](File%20Browser.md#12-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document) asks of it by name: the enumeration walk, the stored-name reading of source names, K7's path identities and K9's read-through. Added 2026-09-04 at the code round, and it is a naming rather than a new service** — [F10a](#62-what-the-adapter-is-for)'s third bullet already places a reading of what the filesystem holds at this adapter, beside the collision judgement, and [F12](#63-what-it-may-not-read-as-state) already binds what such a reading may not be read as. **Without the naming those four reads had no route at all**, because [A1](#4-module-api-boundaries) closes every route to a service this section does not list, which is the same gap [L3a](#51-one-store-and-its-contents-are-another-documents-list) was written to close for `Keys`. **The local record for that document's upload request, whose durable half is one [§9.4](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#94-the-outcome-record) outcome record and never a store of its own ([A2](#4-module-api-boundaries)): the fields its U1 adds to that record are carried here, beside the enumeration and the outcomes §9.4 fixes for every module. Added 2026-09-04 at the code round, on the same ground as the reads above.** The suppression table for its dismissible warnings. **And one class into its order, added 2026-08-29**: the update fetch's admission class ([11.3](#113-the-one-desktop-side-admission-point) R19), ranked **below `upload` and above the preview fetch** by this document's declaration against its O4 list — the route O4's own closing sentence provides — which its declared order and O5's count-scaled share take up like the five it declared, no edit to that list being owed.

**And N8's telling is taken here rather than refused, added 2026-08-29.** That document routed it on the same day and it was carried nowhere, which its [Section 13](File%20Browser.md#13-unresolved) item 17 records and which is closed on this side: removing an upload request record removes with it the provenance of every file that request sent, so **the removal act names that cost before it is taken** — after it, N6's lookup finds nothing and N7 answers *no record* for a file Smoozy itself uploaded. **The lifetime is not moved to buy it back**, that being a closed input's — `State, identity and recovery` §9.4 declares the outcome record user-held and §11.1 places it in the class that ends when the user removes it and never otherwise. **What this document fixes is what the telling is not**, the wording and the placement being Design's. It is **not a dismissible warning and takes no suppression row**: `File Browser` M3 declares one row, `file-browser.delete`, and nothing else there is dismissible, and [G4](#152-the-warning-identity)'s row carries no per-subject qualifier that could hold this one. It is **not a failure record and raises none** ([§8.1](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#81-two-kinds-and-the-kind-is-declared)), nothing having failed. And it is **not a box**: `Reporting a problem` C2 fixes that exactly one interruption exists outside the failure shape, that it is the crash offer, and that **no second thing may ever cite that clause as precedent**. So it is prose said at the moment the user asks, on the shape N7a already uses for the same record's absence — *"said in as many words at the start, beside what it costs"* — reaching the screen through [X7](#173-what-it-must-never-touch)'s typography point like every other piece of prose a user reads.

**To `Servers` and `Keys`.** The secret adapter and its helper, and the module boundary that means none of them holds a secret value ([Section 9](#9-the-secret-adapter-and-its-helper)). The name-reading adapter, which is the only reader of the computer name and the login account name for Keys ([Section 10](#10-the-application-identity-adapter)). **And, for `Keys`, the two readings of [5.1](#51-one-store-and-its-contents-are-another-documents-list) L3a, supplied 2026-08-29 and routed in from [Keys](Keys.md#13-unresolved) §13 item 20**: the **user home directory** reading its L1a asks for, and the placement, protection and start-of-run sweep of **Smoozy's own temporary area** its T2 writes into. **Until that date this handover named neither, and the route was recorded on `Keys`' side alone** — which is why the item outlived a stage close while its six siblings closed. For `Servers`: the connection condition is presented from the record it supplies, and the notification kind for a connection Smoozy could not restore is raised from that record and not from a request failing (T13). **And the repairing server's presentation is [16.7](#167-row-347-presented-a-repair-that-says-when-it-next-tries)'s**: three readings handed over and never four, the work column's vocabulary held apart from K6's three observed values, a countdown permitted as a drawing under P10b, and the one-slot collision between *Rendering* and *Reconnecting…* left to Design with both readings supplied.

**To `Queue & History`.** The notification service and, specifically, **the boundary between *now* and *catching up*** — everything applied at [§7](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#7-reconciliation-on-the-desktop) step 3 is its catch-up notice's and posts nothing ([13.5](#135-telling-now-from-catching-up-without-a-clock)). That **a batch of scene-jobs speaks once**, composed from the group identity carried on its own job records and needing no new store (T11) — **and that the group speaks only where at least one member finished, a left-stopped member still counting towards the last** (T27). The fetched derivative is what a finished-job notification attaches and nothing is made on this side (T15). The presentation of a job's ending, its raw record and its four collection outcomes ([16.3](#163-row-286-presented-the-plain-sentence-and-the-machines-own-words)).

**To `Job files and outputs`.** The receiving-filesystem collision judgement, which its N14 names as what stands between the inventory's names and the loss observed on 2026-08-17. The local lifetimes it already cites are `State, identity and recovery`'s and are not restated here.

**To every document that will be written after this one — five rules that bind them** *(four until 2026-08-25, when the diary's declaration joined)*. No module keeps a durable store of its own (A2). Every dismissible warning's suppression is one row in the one table and a module declares its warning identities (G3, A7). Every string of prose a user reads goes through the typography point and every string that is data goes by the verbatim route instead, those two being the complete set (X7, X9). **Every local write goes through the platform file adapter** (A4), because a write by any other route is outside Row 303 entirely. And **every module declares its diary acts — the fixed line form and the identities each carries — and a module with no declaration writes nothing to the diary at all** (A7's fifth, `Reporting a problem` J2), because a diary filled to each module's own taste reads as complete and is not.

## 22. Unresolved

Each entry names what is not established, what breaks, and what holds without it. **The list is long and that is the correct outcome**: the Windows third of nearly everything is documentation alone, the Linux desktop third is documentation alone too, and **no desktop edition of either system has ever been reached** — four Windows machines have been rented and every one reported a Server edition, and no Linux desktop computer has been run at all (items 2 and 3, [Y3](#what-binds-this-before-anything-is-decided)). *(Corrected 2026-08-31; this read **"and there is no computer for either"**, which its own item 2 and Y3 already contradicted for Windows. **The precision is the point rather than a reversal**: a Windows machine can be rented and four have been, so a Windows question is untried rather than untestable, while a Windows **desktop-edition** question is still out of reach of what has been rented.)* **Three numbers are absent from the list and nothing withheld them. Recorded 2026-09-02:** the entries run 1 to 72 — item 64 was added later the same day and item 65 the same day again, both after this note was first written as *1 to 63*; items 66 and 67 on 2026-09-06 at Build, and items 68 to 72 on 2026-09-12 at Build — with **items 7, 24 and 29 missing** — no duplicate, no gap in the entries themselves — and the three numbers were skipped in composition rather than struck, deleted or reserved. **No entry was ever written under any of them**, nothing anywhere in `Preparation/` or `Audits/` cites this section at item 7, 24 or 29, and the head count of twenty-seven rows is unaffected. **They are not renumbered**, because every citation of items 1 to 63 across the stage points at a number rather than at a position, and this note is the record item 45's practice requires — *"This entry stays in the list as a closed one rather than being deleted"* — applied to a number that never carried an entry. **None of them stopped this document**, and **the seams at items 31, 32, 34 and 42 are disagreements with closed inputs rather than gaps, recorded here as this document's own rule requires and repaired in neither direction**; [Section 23](#23-moments-that-reached-the-creator) records what was checked, including the one answer that reached the Creator in Round 1 and the ruling it came back with.

**PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, 2026-09-02 — every Windows reading in this list, and the Linux-desktop readings with them.** The Creator ruled on **2026-08-31** that there will be **no further Blender research and no further rented machine for answering a question**. So no item below that waits on a Windows machine or on a Linux desktop computer is pending: **it is permanently unresolved, and no reader may leave a space in the code or in the record for an answer that is coming.** *(Build may rent a machine to run the app on. That was ruled the same day, it is a different act, and it revives nothing here.)* **This document reaches nine of the eleven readings on that standing Windows list**, and each is written that way at the item that carries it: the four name rules Windows returns nothing established for (item 63), the long-path prefix (item 62), the detached-volume error (item 9), the publication rename (item 4), the reputation warning (item 6), and the stored capitalisation (item 10). **A tenth left that list by being answered rather than abandoned** — whether Windows localises the on-disk downloads name, read 2026-08-22 and negative ([W6](#8-where-a-download-is-proposed-to-land), item 20) — and the two the list still holds are `Keys`' and `Servers`'. **Every one of the nine states what breaks and what holds without it, and none of them is waiting.**

requirements that do not depend on the platform: staging on the same filesystem, and no route to the final name but the publication step. **PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, 2026-09-02, on all three systems.** [F18](#65-the-publication-operation-and-what-would-demonstrate-it)'s throwaway wants a computer running each system — a desktop Linux computer this project does not have, and a Windows machine for the Windows half — and the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31 means none is coming. **This is not a pending item and must not be read as one.** **The silence is already removed and it costs no reading of its own:** F18 states in the answer's own body that the operation is **not claimed demonstrated** and names what a negative result would look like, so no builder reads the chosen operation as an observed one; and [C13](#73-the-taken-check-is-an-exclusive-create-and-the-filesystem-answers-it) makes the one window a user could meet recoverable rather than silent — the transfer record is durable in *publication pending*, naming both the staging path and the final path, before the first step of publication runs, so a crash inside the window leaves a file the record names and the next run either completes the publication or removes the reservation.
5. **Whether a quarantined macOS app re-triggers first-open approval for the copy it writes for itself.** Not documented on any first-party page read; the checker attacked it and could not close it, every apparent answer being a forum, which the bar excludes. **Breaks:** if it re-triggers, macOS users meet an approval dialog on **every** update — which converts *updating is not something they have to remember* into exactly that, and **that is a change to what the user gets and returns to Specs at the moment the fact closes**. **What clearing that dialog costs the user is unestablished too — corrected 2026-08-22, out of the first sanity sweep's verification round.** This clause used to continue *"and clearing it costs the user their login password"*, flat and unsourced, and no first-party page read says a password is involved: Apple's *Safely open apps on your Mac* (`https://support.apple.com/en-us/102445`, read 2026-08-20) describes the override for a **blocked** app as *"click the Open Anyway button"* and then *"you can click Open"*, names no password anywhere, and is not about the notarized first-open case at all. **So the cost is unestablished in both directions, and this item is open on both halves rather than on the trigger alone.** **One measurement was taken and it does not close either half** — on the Creator's Mac, macOS 26.5.2 build 25F84, 2026-08-20: a file a program fetches over its own connection and writes with ordinary file calls carries `com.apple.provenance` and **not** `com.apple.quarantine`, and the attribute is **not** inherited from a quarantined parent bundle, while browser downloads in the same folder do carry it, so the attribute exists on that machine and the control holds. **Absence of the attribute is not absence of the prompt**: Apple's Gatekeeper page (read 2026-08-20) states *"Gatekeeper also tracks the provenance of files written by downloaded software"* and that *"by default, all software in macOS is checked for known malicious content the first time it's opened, regardless of how it arrived on the Mac"* — a second mechanism on exactly this case, watching the one attribute both tests did find. **What closes it:** a Developer ID-signed, notarized build launched once from a quarantined download, then updated in place from inside itself and re-launched — the Apple Developer Program membership at 99 USD a year that E2 and E6 need anyway, and no second machine. **What holds without it:** Z8's choice — replacing the complete signed bundle at the installed path the user already approved rather than installing one at a new path — which is the arrangement least likely to trip it, and the rest of Row 280, which is unaffected on Linux and unaffected on Windows.
6. **Whether Windows' own reputation warning fires on a build the application launches itself.** The exact twin of item 5, exposed by this document. **Two halves of this entry were stale and are repaired 2026-08-23; the item stays open.** It said the question was *held by no register entry*, and `Facts.md` **Fact 97 holds it** — numbered out of this document and carrying the reading since. And it said flatly that **no page read joins the two**, which Fact 97 records as **corrected on 2026-08-22**: *"at least three first-party Microsoft pages bearing on it were unread"*, and one of them has since been read. **What that reading did and did not do.** *Microsoft Defender SmartScreen overview*, `https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/security/operating-system-security/virus-and-threat-protection/microsoft-defender-smartscreen/`, page dated 2026-04-23, read 2026-08-20, **pulls both ways in its own words** — *"SmartScreen protects against malicious files from the internet. It doesn't protect against malicious files on internal locations or network shares"* against *"It checks any files an app (including 3rd-party browsers and email clients) that attempts to download and run."* **What no page read states is the negative**, that an untagged file is not checked. **So the join is still not made and the item is not closed**; what is repaired is that this entry claimed nothing bearing on it had been read. Microsoft documents the warning for a **newly created binary** and documents the unattended update outcome, and no page read joins those two. **Breaks:** if it fires, Windows users meet a warning on every update and Row 280's *not something they have to remember* is narrowed there too. **What holds without it:** nothing in the mechanism — Z13's side-by-side shape is documented either way — and the side-by-side shape **cannot** take Z8's precaution, because the new build is at a new path by construction (Z17). **PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, 2026-09-02, and both routes to it are spent.** The reading route has produced two Microsoft pages pulling opposite ways in their own words, and **no page read states the negative** — that an untagged file is not checked; the throwaway route wants a Windows computer, and the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31 means none is coming. **This is not a pending item and must not be read as one.** **The silence is already removed:** [19.2](#192-signing-and-where-the-cost-lands) states the Windows cost plainly rather than letting the download promise be read as promising a silent first run — E8 that a signature does not buy silence, and **E8a that on Windows 11 the ending may not be a warning at all but a block**, which is different in kind and is Microsoft's own sentence rather than an inference from this one — and Z17 states that the side-by-side shape cannot take the macOS precaution.
8. **The mount-point-survives case is not defended** (F14). Where a mount point survives a detach, `open()` succeeds and the file lands on the underlying volume **with no error anywhere**, and F1's re-check by path cannot catch it. **Breaks:** a download reported as written to the user's drive is written to another one. **What holds without it:** the file is whole and is at the path the user chose; what is wrong is which disk it is on. Reproduced twice each way and **neither behaviour adjudicated as the normal case**.
9. **Windows' detached-volume error is not established** — six candidate codes exist and Microsoft's `WriteFile` page names none — **and Linux's manual pages name none either**. **Breaks:** F3's per-system classification on those two systems, **and F4a's fourth class with it, which is macOS-observed and is claimed for neither of the other two (added 2026-08-23)**. **What holds without it:** F1 and F2, which do not depend on which value comes back. **PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, 2026-09-02.** The Windows half wants a Windows machine and the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31 means none is coming; the Linux half wants a manual page that names the value and no page read names one, so neither half is pending. **This is not a pending item and must not be read as one.** **The silence is already removed by [F1](#61-the-one-rule-the-whole-adapter-is-built-on) and [F4a](#62-what-the-adapter-is-for), and it costs no reading of its own:** no return value is an outcome on any of the three systems — the destination is re-checked by path after closing and **that** re-check is what is reported — and a value this project has not read is reported as **a write that failed with no cause named**, which is F4a's own discipline for the one value it does classify, rather than as a class composed for it. **What is never done is the opposite:** no per-system classification is asserted on Windows or Linux from the macOS values, and nothing infers a detached volume, a full disk or a permission refusal from an unrecognised error.
10. **Windows has a documented stored-capitalisation route on NTFS and ReFS, but it is unobserved and other filesystems refuse it.** `FileNormalizedNameInformation`, reached through `GetFileInformationByHandleEx` with `FileNormalizedNameInfo`, returns the exact casing stored on disk on NTFS and ReFS; other filesystems return `STATUS_INVALID_DEVICE_REQUEST` (Fact 121). **NTFS's folding rule and directory-enumeration casing remain undocumented, and nobody has run the call. Breaks:** [7.4](#74-which-name-is-in-the-way) on a Windows filesystem other than NTFS or ReFS — the outcome says *taken* without saying *taken by a name that differs only in capitals* — and the same distinction on NTFS or ReFS if the unrun throwaway contradicts the publication. **What holds without it:** Row 303 itself, because C10 asks the filesystem rather than modelling it and the exclusive create fails whatever the volume's folding rule is. **PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, 2026-09-02.** Running the call wants a Windows computer, and the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31 means none is coming; NTFS's folding rule is undocumented on every page read, so no reading closes the other half either. **This is not a pending item and must not be read as one.** **The silence is already removed by [C18](#74-which-name-is-in-the-way), and it costs no reading of its own:** where the call refuses it refuses **loudly** with `STATUS_INVALID_DEVICE_REQUEST`, so a filesystem that cannot answer says so rather than returning a wrong spelling, and the outcome handed to `Download` says *taken* without saying *taken by a name that differs only in capitals* — a stated limit rather than a false guarantee, and never a spelling composed to fill the gap.
11. **No notification was posted and no permission was requested, deliberately, because the machine is the Creator's.** **Breaks:** image rendering, banner placement and the exact error returned when a request has been denied are **unobserved even on macOS**. **What holds without it:** the preconditions of T2, which were observed to the point of the process being killed, and the permission model of T6, which is documented.
12. **On Linux there may be no notification service at all, and Smoozy cannot fix it.** The specification says clients *"must not assume that it is available."* **Breaks:** all four kinds on such a computer. **What holds without it:** every one of the four also leaves its own durable in-app record (T22), and it is written as a case rather than as a failure (T5).
13. **The Linux picture has no capability string and the Windows picture rests on two Microsoft pages that disagree.** **Breaks:** on Linux the picture may be dropped silently and nothing detects it; on Windows it is unresolved which source works for an unpackaged app. **What holds without it:** the notification still says the job finished, which is the kind's own content, and macOS's route is fully documented.
14. **CLOSED — already answered on 2026-08-21 and this entry failed to reach it; confirmed at the Architecture seam close, 2026-08-22.** `State, identity and recovery` §1.1 names per-transfer records nested under their transfer arrangement or multi-part action outcome, cleanup debts included, and §11.1's user-held row gives those parent records their lifetime, the nested records going with them. `Server protocol and transfers` §12 item 13 closed on the same ground the same day. **Nothing further is owed and nothing was written for this item.** The original entry follows, unchanged. **The per-transfer record has no home in the local record's inventory, and that inventory is another document's.** `Server protocol and transfers` S6 and S9 put a durable per-transfer record in the local record; [§1.1](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#11-on-the-users-computer)'s inventory names *transfer arrangements* and no per-transfer record, and §8.7 says *"an ad-hoc upload from the file browser is not a transfer arrangement"*. **That document already recorded it as a seam** (its §12 item 13) and it lands here because **this document builds the store**. **It is not decided here**: that inventory's one home is §1.1 and writing it a second time would give a fact two homes. **Breaks:** a store built to §1.1 as it stands has nowhere for *durable before the first byte is written* to land, and with it Row 197 and C11's resume; and it wants a lifetime class in §11.1, which nothing assigns. **What holds without it:** the record's shape and contents, both settled in S9.

    **A SECOND SEAM LANDED HERE 2026-09-18, and this item is [L1](#51-one-store-and-its-contents-are-another-documents-list)'s standing home for the class rather than one entry that closed.** Written by the builder of Build step 10b piece 6, under the build runner's ruling of 2026-09-17 — `Audits/Build/Step 10b/Ruling — where the machine's per-job note lands on the desktop.md` — which is where the reasoning is and which this does not restate. **No auditor has seen this text.**

    **What the ruling settled.** [Queue & History](Queue%20&%20History.md#10-the-note-the-figure-and-what-the-read-fills-in) N1's per-job note is written by the supervisor on the machine — the label, the started and ended moments on the machine's clock, the ending, the attempt count and re-run marks, *given up* where it was, and N6's last-frame figure — and [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#7-reconciliation-on-the-desktop) §7 V1 step 4 has the desktop fill in from those notes what the local record does not hold. **`LocalJobRecord` had a field for none of it**, so the carried half either became fields of that record or a second kind beside it. **It is fields of the local job record. No new record kind and no new store.**

    **Why it is recorded here and not written into the inventory.** [L1](#51-one-store-and-its-contents-are-another-documents-list) forbids this document adding a row to [§1.1](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#11-on-the-users-computer)'s inventory and says a mechanism needing a record that inventory does not name is recorded as a seam here. **This adds no row**: that inventory already names *"job records — provisional and confirmed"*, and adding fields to a kind it names is not a new row — which is the same shape J1's own *"per-job resolved set as a field of the job's own record rather than a sixth kind"* already has. **It is recorded because it is a seam**: a fact that crosses from one document's store to another's record, and a later reader of §1.1 should be able to find where the note's half went.

    **Breaks:** nothing. **What holds:** the shape and contents are `Queue & History`'s entire, at that document's N1 to N22; the store is this document's, built at record version 23 in `src/record/job_records.rs`; and the lifetime is the local job record's own, which [§11.1](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#111-five-classes-and-the-reason-there-is-no-sixth) already gives it.
15. **PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, closed 2026-10-16 on the build runner's ruling R6 of that day (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/The runner's rulings.md`), carried by owed piece 9 of Build step 10c: the automatic trigger does not start a supervisor whose own restarter has given up, and this document does not route it through `Server Setup`'s start step.** **What breaks, in three points.** **The case is narrow**: a supervisor already running lives through its mount filling (`Rented-server runtime` §16 item 25), so the trigger never meets this condition in the ordinary shape of a full disk; what is left is a supervisor that had to start, or restart, on a mount already at zero, on the systemd kind. **The way out is the user's**: `Server Setup`'s user-facing fallback re-run issues the plain start of the registered service where the package is healthy and the supervisor dead (`App/Server Setup/src/fallback.rs`), which is the one start command Batch E observed recovering the machine completely, and it keeps `Rented-server runtime` §3.2's one restarter. **A second, automatic start cannot be proved without a rental**, and nothing rents one to answer a question. **So the Breaks and What holds below stand as written**, and nothing is owed to this item. *(The entry before this close follows, unchanged.)* **What performs the act that starts a supervisor whose own restarter has given up.** `Rented-server runtime` §16 item 12 names this document's trigger and `Server Setup` as where the recovery act sits and **deliberately does not answer it**, because a second restarter on the machine would be a second restarter. **What this document answers is the reaching**: [Section 12](#12-the-automatic-supervisor-unavailable-repair-trigger) fires the bootstrap-and-repair request at every command-path attempt that finds the supervisor still silent, on the pre-package surface, which reaches a machine whose supervisor is dead. **What it does not answer is what the machine does with it in that condition**, which is that document's §2. **Breaks:** on a systemd machine whose state mount filled, the machine stays dead until `Server Setup`'s user-facing re-run, even after the user frees the space. **What holds without it:** the trigger reaches the machine, the disk-full diagnosis is presented, the held job stayed held throughout the observation, and the machine recovers completely on one plain start command.
16. **Fact 96's figures are worth exactly their own date, 2026-08-18.** **Breaks:** nothing in the choice, which rests on the ordering rather than on any figure. **What holds without it:** H6's properties, which are documentation rather than measurement, and H7's fallback order, which is re-derivable from any later reading.
17. **Installed and unpacked sizes are documented by none of the six candidates.** **Breaks:** any claim about what Smoozy occupies once installed, which this document therefore does not make. **What holds without it:** the download size, which is what the Creator's ruling is about.
18. **The shared rate budget was exercised on uploads only, and the download half of a budget changed while channels are moving stands unrun.** *(**Corrected 2026-09-02.** This read *"and a budget changed while channels are moving has never been run"*, which [`Server protocol and transfers` R16](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#85-what-is-not-observed-and-it-is-named-here-rather-than-in-a-footnote) withdrew on **2026-08-22** and which this document's own [R16](#114-the-achieved-rate-reading) was corrected against on 2026-08-31; the item was not brought with it.)* **The mid-flight change is observed on the upload side** — 2026-08-22, four runs under two governors, a cumulative governor overshooting on a raise and undershooting on a lowering while a bounded five-second window landed both changes inside the minute each fell in (`Server protocol and transfers` R16, and its §12 item 6, whose upload half closes with it). **The download half is a different question rather than the same one twice**, because the download case differs materially — *"a receiver enforces a cap by not reading, so buffering can let the wire rate overshoot"*. **PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, 2026-09-02.** Running the download half wants a rented machine to draw bytes from, and the Creator ruled on **2026-08-31** that there will be no further rented machine for answering a question — so **it is not a pending item, no reader may leave a space in the code or in the record for the run, and Row 306's downward half stands on the mechanism permanently.** **Breaks:** the fidelity of the download cap to the wire, and Row 306's *yes* as **observed upwards and reasoned downwards** rather than observed in both directions. **What holds without it:** the shape of the mechanism, which is the same in both directions; R6b's bounded averaging window, which that run turned from an argument into a requirement and which [R15a](#114-the-achieved-rate-reading) takes whole; and the reading of [11.4](#114-the-achieved-rate-reading), which is a count of bytes Smoozy handled and is labelled as such.
19. **What each of the three system web views does with the two typography substitutions, and whether every design value the mockup carries renders on the frozen engine.** **Breaks:** a line that breaks where Design said it would not, or a value that draws differently on one of three engines. **What holds without it:** X4 — both rules are characters in the string rather than layout instructions, so nothing about them depends on a stylesheet feature — and H12, which forbids building an interface behaviour on a capability not present on all three.
20. **Whether the macOS downloads route looks the location up or composes it**, which wants a compiled Foundation call. **Breaks:** W1's macOS row if the route composes and the user has relocated the folder. **What holds without it:** W2 and W3, which are the two rules that actually protect the user's own choice. **The Windows half of this item is removed, 2026-08-23, because it was answered rather than abandoned.** It read *"and **whether Windows localises the on-disk name**, which wants a Windows computer"*; the input document withdrew that on 2026-08-22 and Microsoft answers it in a page — `KNOWNFOLDER_DEFINITION`'s `pszRelativePath` *"refers to the physical file system path, and is not localized"* (`https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/shobjidl_core/ns-shobjidl_core-knownfolder_definition`, page dated 2018-12-05, read 2026-08-23). [W6](#8-where-a-download-is-proposed-to-land) carries the strike and what it changes, which is nothing but the count here.
21. **RE-WRITTEN 2026-09-06 onto the shell that was actually chosen. The entry as it stood was entirely about the withdrawn shell's beta notice, which is a risk in a thing Smoozy does not use — noise a later reader would have spent time on.** **The selected shell publishes no next-version notice at all, and it does publish a next major version as a work list with no date.** The whole reading, with every address and the date read, is [H10a](#23-the-two-negatives-that-bear-on-row-280-weighed-rather-than-noted). What is left unestablished is **when a next major version of the selected shell arrives and what it changes** — its own milestone carries no due date and no changelog, and no page read states either. **Breaks:** nothing today. The released version is 2.11.5, [E4](#191-what-each-system-is-built-as) and [H6b](#22-the-route-chosen-and-the-properties-it-is-held-to) are written on what 2.11.5 publishes, and nothing in this document rests on a facility announced for a version that has not shipped. **The one place a later major version would land is E4's Linux row**: the milestone's open item *"feat(linux): migrate to GTK4 and WebKitGTK 6.0"* is a move off the `libgtk-3-0` and `libwebkit2gtk-4.1-0` pair E4 declares, so a package built on it would declare a different pair and the release matrix's GTK and WebKitGTK halves would be re-derived a second time. **What holds without it:** every clause here, because each is written against a published release rather than against an announcement. **What would close it:** the selected project publishing a release, a date or a migration statement for its next major version — **a reading, open and free, wanting no machine and no rental.** *(The original entry follows, unchanged, because it is a true record of what was written on 2026-08-18 against a shell that was then the provisional direction.)*
    ~~**Wails' current major version carries a beta notice for the next one on every page.** **Breaks:** nothing today. **What holds without it:** H10 — nothing in this document rests on a facility announced for a version that has not shipped.~~
22. **Linux's stored-name route is answered for case-folding ext4 and unresolved elsewhere** (C16). Fact 122 establishes that ext4 stores the byte-for-byte name userspace supplied and does not expose or store the folded form. **What remains:** every folding filesystem that is not ext4, which filesystem a distribution presents, and the unrun Linux throwaway. **Breaks:** [7.4](#74-which-name-is-in-the-way) on an unestablished folding filesystem — the outcome says *taken* without saying which stored name took it — and the same distinction on ext4 if the throwaway contradicts the publication. **What holds without it:** Row 303 itself, because C10 asks the filesystem rather than modelling it; the ordinary case-sensitive Linux case; and the documented ext4 case. **What would have closed it:** the Linux desktop throwaway — **permanently unresolved, 2026-09-02**, wanting a Linux desktop computer this project does not have and will not rent (item 3, Y3), not a pending item and no space left for it — **plus** a first-party answer for any supported folding filesystem other than ext4, **which is a reading, is open, and costs nothing.**
23. **CLOSED by the Creator on 2026-08-23: H13 takes Wails' option *Embed*, so a Windows machine with no suitable runtime is offered the embedded bootstrapper rather than told the application cannot run.** In their own words, *"150 KB and you're bothering to ask me? of course it's okay."* **What the ruling reaches is one system of three, and H13a states it**: Windows closed on both halves of the condition, absent and too old; Linux never had this case, because [E4](#191-what-each-system-is-built-as)'s declared dependencies and [Z10](#203-linux-a-package-transaction)'s package transaction meet the Ubuntu-server case Canonical's manifest establishes before there is anything to launch, and item 45's ruling of 2026-08-22 already settled what that route costs the user; macOS cannot have the absent case at all. **No spec sentence moves and nothing returns to Specs** — the ruling restores the promise Row 282 already makes rather than granting the user something new. **What is left of the question is at items 48 and 49** and is not this entry. **The original entry follows, unchanged.** **Whether every supported Mac, Windows and Linux configuration carries a system web view current enough for the interface.** H13 declines both documented options that would grow the artifact back toward engine scale, and its answer where a system's web view is absent or too old is that **the application says so rather than running**. **Nothing establishes that no supported configuration is such a machine**, and **there is no computer to pose it on for two of the three** (items 2 and 3). **Breaks:** Row 282 on such a machine, where the user meets a downloaded binary that starts and says it cannot run, on a system Row 282 says Smoozy runs on. **What holds without it:** the ruling's own comparison, which is against real release artifacts and is unaffected; and H6's properties, every one of which is checkable against Wails' own documentation rather than against a user's machine. **Updated 2026-08-21, and it now reaches the Creator.** The reason it did not was that the cost of the alternative was unestablished. **Wails establishes it: option Embed adds *"~150k to the binary size"*** and offers to run the official bootstrapper (H13). So the choice is no longer a hypothesis, and it is filed to the Creator rather than declined here. **And what Linux carries is now established too, on Canonical's own image manifests read 2026-08-20**: the Ubuntu 24.04.3 **desktop** manifest lists `libgtk-3-0t64`, `libwebkit2gtk-4.1-0` and `libwebkitgtk-6.0-4`, while the **server** manifest of 689 packages contains no `libwebkit*` and no `libgtk-3-0` at all — **so the mainstream Linux desktop does carry it out of the box and a supported Linux install can lack it entirely.** *(A flag, not a finding: Ubuntu 24.04 desktop ships `libwebkit2gtk-4.1-0`, not 4.0, and Wails' installation page names a build tag for that — a build-flag question Build meets either way.)* **If the fact closes the other way it is a change to what the user gets and returns to Specs then**, in the same words items 5 and 6 use; [Section 23](#23-moments-that-reached-the-creator) records the check.
25. **The frame preview is narrowed in two ordinary cases** ([13.4](#134-the-frame-preview-and-it-is-three-answers)). The fetched derivative exceeds macOS's **10 MB** attachment ceiling and the notification is posted without a picture (T16); and the derivative is not yet on disk when the finish is learned, where T17 refuses to delay, suppress or re-post. **Nothing establishes that a fetched derivative is by construction inside the ceiling.** **Breaks:** Row 284's preview clause — *"a finished-job notification carries that job's frame preview where the job has one"* — in both cases, and the row's own reason for the picture is that it *"is the picture that says the render happened"*. **What holds without it:** the notification still says the job finished, which is the kind's own content; the derivative is on the job record and the picture is reachable in the app; and T17's refusal to put a notification behind a fetch, which is what keeps the *now* half of the same row.
26. **A job that ends inside any reconnection window produces no notification, however brief the outage and however recent the event.** T20 makes every reconnection re-run [§7](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#7-reconciliation-on-the-desktop) step 3, so everything learned there is catch-up; Fact 57 makes the window routine rather than rare, a caller's own line pulled going undetected for **21 minutes 45 seconds** with keepalives off and **56 seconds** with them on. **Breaks:** Row 284's first kind in the case the row was written for — a job finishing while the user works in Blender — with the substitute being the in-app record, which is where the user is not looking. **What holds without it:** T22, every one of the four kinds also leaving its own durable in-app record; and T19's refusal to use a clock, which stands and is not reopened. **What would close it:** a clock-free boundary drawn on `Servers`' own definition of a connected period, which cannot be asserted from inside this document.
27. **Whether replacing a notification carrying an identity already used re-alerts the user, or restores one the user dismissed, is not established on any of the three.** What Fact 73 gives is that replace-by-identity **exists** on all three and is **atomic on Linux by specification**; atomicity of the replacement is a different property from whether the replacement re-banners, re-sounds or brings back a dismissed notification. **Nothing was posted on any system** (item 11), so even the success path is unobserved, and this sits beside that item. **Breaks:** Row 288 on the one surface that reaches a user working in Blender, on all three systems at once — a hundred files refused by one full mount resolve to one condition identity, and a replace that re-alerts turns *said once* into a hundred banners, which is the row's own named harm. **What holds without it:** Q9's in-app half, untouched; and **Q13**, which is what removes the dependence — a later failure resolving to a standing identity needs no post at all, so the answer holds whichever way this closes.
28. **Interior sentence endings inside a multi-sentence string handed over as one string are not bound** ([17.1](#171-one-application-point-and-two-substitutions) X2, X10). The point binds the last inter-word space of the string it is handed and does not parse, deliberately (X10). **Breaks:** the second half of Row 304's rule — *"the last two words of any piece of prose are bound together, so a sentence never ends with one word alone on a line"* — wherever a module hands over a paragraph rather than a sentence. **What holds without it:** the em-dash rule entire, the orphan rule at every string's end, and X10's route to the whole rule, which costs a caller one decision and puts no model inside the point.
30. **The reservation's window, which the Creator has ruled acceptable and which still costs what it costs.** Between C10's exclusive create and the publishing rename a **zero-byte** file carrying the frame's exact name stands in the folder the user chose, and where Smoozy is stopped in that instant it is left behind until the next run clears it. **The Creator ruled on 2026-08-18 that this is acceptable**, `Download` carries the user-visible consequence, and [Section 23](#23-moments-that-reached-the-creator) records the verdict — **so this is not an open decision.** What is carried here is what it costs: **Breaks:** nothing of Row 303, which C10 keeps on all three; what it costs is a file in the user's own folder that nothing about it says Smoozy put there, which they may open and find empty, or delete and thereby delete the thing holding the name. **What holds:** the record is in *publication pending* naming both paths before the first step runs, so the file is never unrecorded and the next run either completes the publication or removes it (C13).
31. **CLOSED against `Server protocol and transfers` on 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close; the shape's half is still owed and rides the shape's own Due-now item.** T5a states that the two points have different scopes and why: the per-server point counts every session because `MaxSessions` is one pool per connection, the desktop-side point holds down line contention and therefore admits what moves bytes, and gating a command behind desktop-wide transfer load would defeat the very reserve T5 keeps. R11 and R18 are unchanged. The original entry follows, unchanged. **Seam — R11 against `Server protocol and transfers` T5 and [The shape](The%20shape.md#7-desktop-app-and-delivery) section 7.** T5 reads *"Every session, and not only the transfer channels"*, and the shape calls the desktop point the one *"through which every per-server session-admission point `Server protocol and transfers` defines is admitted"*; R11 admits transfer channels and the update fetch, and does not gate the command or reading channels. **R18 states how this document reads T5** — as binding the per-server point, which counts all three kinds against one `MaxSessions` — **and the decision itself is kept**, because gating a command behind desktop-wide transfer load would put correlation on every server at once behind the very thing T5's one-session reserve — or, at a cap of one, its alternating lease — protects. **Breaks:** nothing in the mechanism; what is unresolved is that this narrows two closed documents' plain wording and neither is cited as agreeing. **What holds without it:** R12, which leaves the per-server point untouched and `Server protocol and transfers`'; and R13, since either point may refuse. **It is a seam rather than a defect and is not repaired here**, in either direction — the wording is to be made to agree on all three sides.

What is unresolved is that two closed documents disagree in plain text about who may create a final name. **What holds without it:** C13's declared window and its durable pre-state, and item 30's record of what the window costs. **It is a seam rather than a defect and is not repaired here.**
