the three either writes a second answer to a question already answered or routes it back here, which is the two-answers-that-drift fault this stage's rule 4 exists against. **What holds without it:** L3a, Z21b and Section 21's N8 paragraph, each complete on this side and none of them waiting on the other document's record. **What closes it:** one CLOSED line in each of the three, in the form those documents already use.

61. **Whether a posted notification can be taken down at all is not established on Windows or Linux. It is established on macOS: it can.** *(Narrowed 2026-09-09 at Build step 10a piece G, which is the first time anything in Smoozy posted a notification. This had read *"not established on any of the three systems"*. A delivered notification was removed by its own identifier and the removal named that post and nothing else, read out of the system's own log. **T24a stays attempted and forgotten regardless** — the other two systems are unchanged and nothing was built on the answer.)* Added 2026-09-01 with [T24a](#136-a-notification-is-never-relied-on). **Nothing had ever been posted on any system when this was written** — item 11 above — so removal was unobserved exactly as replacement's re-alerting behaviour still is (item 27), and Fact 73 answers the *posting* preconditions and stops there: what it gives is that **replace-by-identity exists** on all three and is atomic on Linux by specification, which is a different operation from withdrawing a post outright. **This item is written as permanently unresolved rather than as needs-research**, per the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31 that no further research and no further rental will happen; a desktop machine of each kind would settle it, and none is coming. **Breaks:** where the removal cannot be made, the operating system's notification centre goes on holding *your connection could not be restored* for a server that has been connected and rendering for a quarter of an hour, which is the exact harm `Whole app` names in its own words — and on `Servers` N2a's 105-second raise that is **every** provider reboot longer than about two and a half minutes, not an occasional one. **What holds without it:** [16.2](#162-row-285-presented-a-live-condition-and-a-recorded-ending) M4's in-app withdrawal, which is unaffected and is what carries the spec's promise; the user is one glance from the truth and nothing is lost. **What would close it:** one post and one removal on each remaining system, or each system's own published documentation of the operation, read under the same rule the posting preconditions were. **That is exactly what closed the macOS third**, and neither of the other two has a machine here.

62. **Windows' long-path prefix is documented and unrun, and what a path written under it looks like to a program that does not use it is stated by nobody.** Added 2026-09-02. **This is Windows reading 6 of the standing eleven, and it is the one no unresolved item in any answer document named** — [C7](#72-the-name-check-before-the-transfer) carried the documented behaviour with no entry here. What is documented is that **Windows silently strips trailing periods and spaces from any path not prefixed with exactly `\\?\`** — Microsoft's own page, no error, the file landing under a different name. **Nobody has run it on a Windows computer**, and no page read states what an ordinary Windows program that does not use the prefix sees at a path Smoozy wrote under it. **PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, 2026-09-02**, per the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31 that there will be no further rental; **this is not a pending item and must not be read as one.** **Breaks:** nothing in [7.2](#72-the-name-check-before-the-transfer)'s outcome, and one thing beyond this document's reach — whether a frame written under the prefix is afterwards reachable by the user's own file manager and by Blender under the name Smoozy reports, which no publication read establishes either way. **What holds without it, and it is the silence-removal C7 already makes at the clause rather than in a list:** **a name that would be silently transformed is refused by [C4](#72-the-name-check-before-the-transfer) rather than written**, so the failure is loud by construction and no frame is quietly renamed on any of the three systems; C5's length check is made per component in the receiving system's own unit; and C10 keeps Row 303 whatever the volume does.

63. **What Windows returns for a name macOS refuses with a visible hard error is claimed by nobody.** Added 2026-09-02, carrying the four name rules [C8](#72-the-name-check-before-the-transfer) leaves open — the forbidden character, the reserved stem, the unpaired surrogate and the over-long component. **These are Windows readings 2 to 5 of the standing eleven and had no entry of their own here.** C8's own words: *"Nothing is claimed about what Windows returns: no page read states it and nothing was measured."* **PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, 2026-09-02**, per the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31; **this is not a pending item and must not be read as one.** **Breaks:** the *which of the three* half of [C4](#72-the-name-check-before-the-transfer)'s refusal on Windows — the refusal itself stands, but whether it can be reported as *not representable*, *too long in this system's own unit* or *silently transformed* depends on values nobody has read — and with it `Download` P4/P6's per-file honesty on Windows, which therefore rests on C7's and C18's documented halves rather than on a measured error set. **What holds without it:** [C3](#72-the-name-check-before-the-transfer)'s moment, fixed by Fact 24 and not by any error value; **C5's length check, which is arithmetic in the receiving system's own unit and needs no error to be made at all**, so the over-long component is caught before the call on all three systems; and item 9's rule for an unread value — a write that failed with no cause named, never a cause composed to fill the gap.

64. **CLOSED the day it was raised, 2026-09-02, and recorded so nobody files it again: the empty server list is Design's and Architecture owes it nothing.** Scenario replay *The machine is destroyed* finding 3 asked whether any architecture document owns `Whole app`'s promise that *"Whenever the server list is empty, it lays out what to do and in what order"*, and named this document as the only candidate owner. **It is not owed here.** The spec's own label on that promise reads **settled at Design** (2026-08-23) and `Preparation/Design/Servers.md` says the promise lands on that screen. **The condition is a count of rows in a store this architecture has already closed** — `Servers` [D6](Servers.md#61-one-durable-store-and-one-row-per-server) is the only route to anything about a server and its enumeration is settled — so the trigger is derived at the draw from that count, holds no state, writes nothing durable, reads no machine, and takes no decision this document would be the one to take. **A clause here would restate a count and name a surface**, which is the ordinary construction [Method](Method.md) puts at Build and the guidance itself is Design's. **Breaks:** nothing built. **What holds without it:** the promise entire, on the two documents that already carry it. **Decided under the Creator's delegation of 2026-09-02** rather than parked, and it is a closure rather than an unresolved item.

65. **CLOSED 2026-09-02 at Build step 1, the same day it was raised, by the one page reading it asked for. The value is `embedBootstrapper`.** The selected shell's configuration reference publishes four values for the installer's web-view step and describes each in its own words: `skip` — *"Do not install the Webview2 as part of the Windows Installer"*; `downloadBootstrapper` — *"Download the bootstrapper and run it. Requires an internet connection. Results in a smaller installer size, but is not recommended on Windows 7"*; **`embedBootstrapper` — *"Embed the bootstrapper and run it. Requires an internet connection. Increases the installer size by around 1.8MB, but offers better support on Windows 7"***; `offlineInstaller` — *"Embed the offline installer and run it. Does not require an internet connection. Increases the installer size by around 127MB"* (`https://v2.tauri.app/reference/config/`, read 2026-09-02; label: **documented**). **`embedBootstrapper` is the ruled one and it matches the ruling on every limb**: it embeds the bootstrapper rather than the runtime, it fetches from Microsoft's servers, and [H13a](#24-what-the-small-shell-costs-and-it-lands-on-the-interface)'s *"the ruling taken on the smaller figure"* is the 1.8 MB this page states rather than the 127 MB of the value that carries the whole runtime. **It is set in the application's own configuration** and the ruling of 2026-08-23 stands unchanged, exactly as H13a said it would. *(The entry as raised follows.)*

    **Which value of the selected shell's installer web-view setting carries the ruled Windows cost.** Added 2026-09-02, when [H5b](#22-the-route-chosen-and-the-properties-it-is-held-to) selected Tauri v2 and [H13a](#24-what-the-small-shell-costs-and-it-lands-on-the-interface)'s own sentence became due — that clause says the equivalent is re-derived from the selected project's publications and the ruling stands unchanged. **The Creator's ruling of 2026-08-23 is that Windows embeds the bootstrapper and the cost is taken; what the ruling never named is an option, and the option name has now changed shells.** **The comparison artifact was built with that step set to skip, deliberately and correctly**, because H5b's own row requires the system web view to be excluded from the measured artifact and named as an external dependency — **a comparison build is not a release build, and nothing about the measurement carries into what ships.** **Breaks:** nothing built, and nothing measured. What is not yet written down is which published value of that setting is the ruled one, so a builder reaching the first Windows release artifact has a ruling and no name to apply it with. **What holds without it:** the ruling itself, unchanged and recorded at H13, and the two limits H13a already carries — that it fetches from Microsoft's servers, and that the two publications price it differently with the ruling taken on the smaller figure. **What closes it:** one reading of that setting's published values on the selected project's own configuration page. **No machine, no rental, no research round** — it is a page, and it is owed before the first Windows release artifact rather than before anything earlier.

67. **On Linux the selected shell cannot tell a call from a frame inside the page apart from a call from the page itself, so [H36](#28-the-privileged-web-content-boundary)'s origin check is unenforceable there and [H33](#28-the-privileged-web-content-boundary)'s `frame-src 'none'` is the only thing left. Added 2026-09-06 with [H31a](#28-the-privileged-web-content-boundary), found at Build.** The shell's own words, *Capabilities*, `https://v2.tauri.app/security/capabilities/`, read 2026-09-06: *"On Linux and Android, Tauri is unable to distinguish between requests from an embedded `<iframe>` and the window itself."* **The shell that did not win published nothing on this either way**, so this is a limit that arrived with the change of shell rather than one that was always there and went unwritten. **Breaks:** nothing today — Smoozy ships no frame, loads no remote document and refuses both at [H31](#28-the-privileged-web-content-boundary) — and what it costs is depth rather than an opening: on Linux a frame that did somehow exist would reach the binding surface as the page, where macOS and Windows would refuse it. **What holds without it:** `frame-src 'none'` and `default-src 'none'` in the shipped policy, proved on a running window at [H33a](#28-the-privileged-web-content-boundary); H31's refusal of remote frames; and [H36](#28-the-privileged-web-content-boundary)'s refusal to compose feature modules at all where the policy cannot be applied. **What this makes true of the code:** `frame-src 'none'` is load-bearing on Linux rather than a second line, so a build that ever relaxes it has removed the whole defence on that system and not one of two. **What would close it:** nothing published closes it — it is the shell's stated behaviour rather than a gap in its pages — so it closes only by that project changing what it does. **It is not on the standing permanently-unresolved Windows list and wants no machine**; it is a limit to be respected rather than a question to be answered, and Linux desktop item 3 bears on watching it, not on establishing it.

> **Items 68 to 72 were added on 2026-09-12 by Build step 10a round 1's upstream must-do U-3**, and they are five readings the notification, clipboard and reveal code rests on that had nowhere on record to sit. **`Preparation/Groundwork/Open Threads.md` was checked first and carries none of them**, and none should go there: that register carries no notification, clipboard or reveal question and this section is where every one of this document's own unresolved readings already lives, so they are written once here and not twice. **On every one of them headcount means nothing** — several models doubting the same thing about an operating system is one training set speaking several times — and **none may be guessed at in the code.** Items 2 and 3 above say why no answer is coming: there is no Windows desktop machine and no Linux desktop computer, and the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31 means none is being rented. **No space is left anywhere for an answer that is not coming.**

68. **Whether the Linux notification server's click signal is addressed to the one client that posted, or broadcast to every client on the session bus, is established by nothing read. Added 2026-09-12, Build step 10a round 1. It sits with item 51.** [T28](#137-what-clicking-a-notification-does) is written on the first reading — the signal goes to the client that posted — and the specification's own sentence is about a connected client rather than about addressing. **Breaks, on the first reading:** a client that has gone by the time the signal is emitted never receives it, so **no click ever reaches Smoozy on that system** and Row 480's *no notification is a dead end* is not kept there. **Breaks, on the second:** a stale entry in Smoozy's own register of the server's numbers can match another application's notification, and the user is sent somewhere they did not ask to go. **What holds either way:** the two repairs Build made on 2026-09-12 are right on both readings and were made for that reason — the post is sent on the same connection the listener holds, so the sending client and the listening client are one client; and an entry leaves the register at the closing signal, so a number is not held after the notification it named is gone. **One reading neither repair covers: whether a server sends the closing signal for a notification it has replaced in place is established by nothing read either.** The listener removes the entry on every closing signal and reads no reason. **Breaks, if it does:** a replaced notification loses its register entry while it is still on screen, so a click on it reaches nothing, and the next replacement is sent with no number to replace and stacks beside it. The notification itself still shows. **No code reads the reason**, because any filter on it would be a guess in code, and none of these may be guessed at. *(Added 2026-09-19 from a code round — the Gemini catch-up of step 10a, Area 4, upstream must-do U-2. This sentence said the repairs were right on both readings without naming this third. No auditor of this document has seen it.)* **PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED**: it wants a Linux desktop computer, and item 3 says why none is coming.

69. **Whether the Windows notification calls require the calling thread's component system to have been started first is established by nothing read, and this is the highest-cost unchecked reading in this section. Added 2026-09-12, Build step 10a round 1. It sits with item 51.** **Breaks:** if they do, **every Windows notification silently does not happen** — the whole of [Section 13](#13-notifications)'s Windows third dead — and **nothing anywhere reports it**, because [T23](#136-a-notification-is-never-relied-on) makes a post attempted and forgotten by design, [T3](#131-the-machinery-each-system-needs)'s shortcut check passes on its own, and the permission read fails into *switched off*, which is an answer rather than a fault. **What holds without it:** nothing, on that system. **What makes the question live rather than theoretical:** two other places in the same application do start it because their own pages demand it — the Start-menu shortcut of [E3](#19-build-outputs-signing-and-hosting) and the folder reveal of [K21](#186-the-folder-reveal-service) — and nothing read says why the notification calls are different. **PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED** as a run; **the documentation route is open and costs nothing** — Microsoft's own pages for those calls can be read at any time, on any machine, and that is this item's one live route.

70. **Whether the Windows shell calls the folder reveal makes accept a path in the long form is established by nothing read. Added 2026-09-12, Build step 10a round 1. It sits with item 53, and it is item 62's question arriving at a second door.** **Breaks:** if they refuse it, the reveal fails on **every** Windows computer every time, and both callers [18.6](#186-the-folder-reveal-service) exists for — `Reporting a problem`'s *the folder has opened* and `Download`'s *show in folder* — read as broken with no cause named and nothing to try. **What holds without it:** [K22](#186-the-folder-reveal-service), every route answering its caller rather than going quiet, which is what makes the failure presentable rather than silent. **PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED** as a run, per items 2 and 3; **the documentation route is open** and is the same one item 62 names.

71. **Whether the Windows notification system accepts an identifier of the length Smoozy uses — a word, a colon and thirty-two characters — is established by nothing read. Added 2026-09-12, Build step 10a round 1. It sits with item 51.** The identifier is [§14](#14-one-repeating-failure-is-said-once) Q10's presentation key, which is composed from identities alone. **Breaks:** if it is refused, replacing a repeating notification stacks a second one beside the first instead of taking its place, and the take-down of [T24a](#136-a-notification-is-never-relied-on) finds nothing to remove — so §14's *said once* is not kept on that system. **What holds without it:** the notification itself still shows, the kind's own content being unaffected. **No code change goes with this, deliberately**: the call that sets the identifier throws its refusal away, and T23 is why — a post is attempted and forgotten with no state depending on the result, and this application has nowhere to put a refusal that is not a branch on it. Noticing it would invite the first caller that acts on it, which is the thing T23 exists to prevent. **PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED** as a run, per item 2; the documentation route is open.

72. **Which thread and which runtime the operating system runs a waited-for shell call on is stated by nothing read, and [3.2](#32-the-interface-thread-and-what-may-never-run-on-it)'s P4 and P5 both depend on the answer while neither cites it. Added 2026-09-12, Build step 10a round 1. It sits with [3.2](#32-the-interface-thread-and-what-may-never-run-on-it) rather than with any other item here.** P5's closed list puts every notification call and every connection off the interface thread, and P4 forbids anything that can block on it — **but both are statements about what Smoozy arranges, and neither says what happens when a waited-for call is made from inside something that is already waiting.** The Linux notification routes and the Linux folder reveal each wait on a runtime from whatever thread called them, and one Linux window-button reading starts another program and waits for it on what may be the drawing thread. **Breaks:** if waiting that way from inside an already-running runtime ends the process rather than merely being slow, a user pressing *show in folder* after a download loses the whole application — every transfer and every render watch with it. **What holds without it:** the window-button reading's own fallback, which draws three buttons at the right when nothing answers, and [K22](#186-the-folder-reveal-service)'s rule that every route answers its caller. **PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED** as a run, per item 3; **the documentation route is open** and is the runtime library's own pages rather than any vendor's.

---

## 23. Moments that reached the Creator

**Four now, and the fourth was ruled on 2026-08-23: a supported machine whose system web view is absent or too old gets the embedded bootstrapper rather than a refusal.** **How it was put:** a user on a supported Mac, Windows or Linux machine whose system web view is absent or too old gets a downloaded Smoozy that starts and says it cannot run; the alternative grows the download by roughly 150 kilobytes and always works. **They ruled the same day, in their own words:** *"150 KB and you're bothering to ask me? of course it's okay."* Label: **Creator-stated** (2026-08-23). **What changed as a result:** [H13](#24-what-the-small-shell-costs-and-it-lands-on-the-interface) takes Wails' option *Embed* where it took option *Error*, the Windows artifact is built with `-webview2 embed`, [H16](#25-the-rulings-stop-run-and-not-reached) no longer records a cost on Row 282, [Section 22](#22-unresolved) item 23 is closed, and Row 282's own answer carries the ruling. **What it did not cost:** no spec sentence moves and nothing returns to Specs — the ruling restores [Whole app](../Specs/Whole%20app.md)'s *"Smoozy runs on Mac, Windows, and Linux"* on a machine the architecture was about to fail it on, rather than granting the user anything new.

**Two things about it are recorded rather than smoothed.** **The ruling was put as *always works* and the shell's own word is *offer*** — *"the application will offer to run the bootstrapper"* (H13) — so what the user meets on such a machine is a prompt to install the runtime, not a silent install; **it is not put back to the Creator**, because it is the documented behaviour of the option they ruled on by name and it is strictly less than the refusal they ruled against, and because the install it leads to needs no administrator (H13a). **And the ruling reaches Windows alone**: Linux never had the case (E4, Z10, item 45) and macOS cannot have its absent half at all, which H13a states and item 48 carries what is left of.

**And the third was ruled on 2026-08-22: the Linux update asks the user for their computer password.** **How it was put:** a Linux user is running Smoozy, a new version is out, Smoozy fetches it the way it does on the other two systems where the user never learns it happened — and on Linux the new version arrives as a distribution package, which is a privileged act, so the update either puts a password box in front of the user or does not happen. **They ruled: ask for the password.** Label: **Creator-stated** (2026-08-22). It is written at [T4](#131-what-a-plain-downloaded-binary-must-carry-before-it-can-post-at-all) and [Z10a](#203-linux-a-package-transaction). **Why it went up rather than being answered here:** [Section 22](#22-unresolved) item 5 had already ruled that the **macOS twin** of this cost returns to Specs at the moment its fact closes, and the Linux fact closed on the mechanism's own documentation. **What it did not cost:** no spec sentence moved — [Z3](#201-what-the-application-does-and-what-the-user-does) already admits a prompt during an update the application itself started, and [Whole app](../Specs/Whole%20app.md)'s own marker reserves *what the user sees of an update, and whether anything is asked of them*; the ruling is recorded there rather than changing a promise. **How it was found:** the third sanity sweep, whose verification round closed the fact — and the clause it contradicted, T4's *"no route asks for administrator"*, had passed every gate this stage has.

**Two before it, and the second was ruled on 2026-08-22: whether a distribution package is the binary the spec promises.** Added that day. **Why it went up rather than being answered here:** [Whole app](../Specs/Whole%20app.md)'s *"The user gets Smoozy by downloading a binary from its website"* is Creator-stated, and E4 had made the Linux artifact a signed, dependency-declaring package installed through the distribution's own package mechanism — which is what makes the runtime library requirements enforceable instead of hoped for. **Only the Creator may say whether that is still the thing their own sentence describes**, and had they said no it would have been a change to what the user gets and back to Specs.

**How it was put, and what they ruled.** In the plainest terms the difference allows: either way the user goes to the website and downloads one file, with no email, no account and no registration; what differs is only what happens when they open it — it runs, or the system installs it and then it runs. And what the package buys is that a machine missing a required library is refused up front with the library named, instead of opening to a blank web view. **They ruled that it counts.** Label: **Creator-stated** (2026-08-22). **No spec sentence moves, nothing returns to Specs, and [Section 22](#22-unresolved) item 45 is closed with the reasoning kept** for the next reader who asks the same question about another platform.

**And one, which did not come from the writing.** Round 1's audit found an answer this section had not put through its own question — the reservation of [7.3](#73-the-taken-check-is-an-exclusive-create-and-the-filesystem-answers-it) C10 — and the round routed it to the Creator, who ruled on **2026-08-18**. It is recorded below as the eleventh checked answer, with the ruling. **Nothing else from this document reached the Creator.**

**Fourteen answers were checked against [Method](Method.md)'s third escalation — does this put anything in front of the user that no spec says they get, a place to look, something to type, or a thing that can be lost — and two of them reach it.** *(This said one; corrected 2026-08-22, because the supported-system web-view answer below now reaches it too. **Both have been filed and both are ruled** — the reservation on 2026-08-18 and the web view on 2026-08-23; updated that day, when the second was filed and came back the same day.)* No gate catches that one, so it was asked of every answer and the verdict is recorded either way. *(Eleven at the writing and at Round 1; the twelfth, thirteenth and fourteenth are Rows 321, 327 and 322, added on 2026-08-21 and all declined — the first two at H24, the third at H30.)*

**Twelve more checks are recorded in their own clauses rather than here, and every one of them is declined. Restated 2026-08-29 at the stage-close repair.** *(The accounting above had not moved since 2026-08-21 while eight of these twelve were written on 2026-08-25 and 2026-08-29 and none was named; and one of the four pointers this paragraph carried named a subsection that held no such check at all.)* [2.2](#22-the-route-chosen-and-the-properties-it-is-held-to) **H6a** (Row 425); [2.7](#27-the-two-faces) **H29a** (Row 483); [3.3](#33-ui-event-delivery) **P11** (Row 476); [11.4](#114-the-achieved-rate-reading) **R15b** (Rows 329 and 426), whose decline covers the R15a reading it is built from; [13.4](#134-the-frame-preview-and-it-is-three-answers) **T17a** (Row 332); [13.7](#137-what-clicking-a-notification-does) **T30** (Row 480); [16.2](#162-row-285-presented-a-live-condition-and-a-recorded-ending) **M7a**; [16.3](#163-row-286-presented-the-plain-sentence-and-the-machines-own-words) **M13b** (Row 504); [16.7](#167-row-347-presented-a-repair-that-says-when-it-next-tries) **M31a** (Row 347); [18.5](#185-the-keyboard-shortcuts-beside-the-menu-items) **K19** (Row 474); [18.6](#186-the-folder-reveal-service) **K24** (Row 500); and [20.1](#201-what-the-application-does-and-what-the-user-does) **Z30**, which carries the check for Row 508's Z26 to Z30 together. **Row 347's was the missing one**: this paragraph named [16.7](#167-row-347-presented-a-repair-that-says-when-it-next-tries) for it from 2026-08-21, M28 to M31 contain no such statement, and **M31a is written on this date rather than the pointer being struck**, so the row keeps a recorded verdict. **The number above is the count recorded in this section and is not the count of answers checked**, which is every answer in this document.

**The Windows signing cost and the warning behind it, which the brief put on the table by name.** Microsoft's own documentation says a signed new binary can still warn until it accumulates *"sufficient evidence of positive reputation"*, taking *"several weeks and hundreds of clean installs from a wide audience"*, with **no mechanism to submit a file for review** and **extended-validation certificates no longer bypassing it** — so at this project's scale the warning does not go away on its own, and it is something the Windows user meets on first run that no spec mentions. **It does not reach the escalation, and the reason is that no permitted answer removes it.** It is a property of Windows met by every downloaded binary of a new publisher; signing does not remove it; the store route that would is **forbidden**, because [Whole app](../Specs/Whole%20app.md) settles that the user downloads a binary from Smoozy's website. **An escalation is for where the architecture's own choice costs the user something; here every choice costs the same, so there is nothing to put to the Creator and nothing for them to rule on.** The identity gate itself — the United States and Canada limit, the tax-history requirement, the mandatory subscription, the 1-to-20-business-day validation, the two Microsoft country lists that disagree, and the "$300–500/year" fallback certificate — **costs the Creator money and the user nothing**, and [The shape](The%20shape.md#the-remote-licence-service) already settled that a cost falling on the Creator rather than the user does not return to Specs. **It is written into [19.2](#192-signing-and-where-the-cost-lands) plainly so that nobody downstream reads the download promise as promising a silent first run.**

**Updated 2026-11-01 with [E7a](#192-signing-and-where-the-cost-lands); no auditor has seen it.** Under SignPath Foundation’s free route the identity gate and the price no longer land on the Creator. **One thing reaches the user that no spec said before: Windows names SignPath Foundation as the publisher.** It needed no escalation, because it entered at Specs first — `Whole app` *Getting the app, and updates* says it, Creator-stated. The verdict above stands: signing still does not buy silence.

**Whether a quarantined macOS app re-triggers first-open approval for the copy it writes for itself, which the brief called the clause most likely to cost the user something nobody wrote down.** **It does not reach the escalation today, and the reason is that the cost is not established rather than that it is small.** If it re-triggers, the user meets an approval dialog on every update, and if clearing that dialog asks for their login password that is exactly *something to type* — but no first-party page read says it re-triggers, **and none says a password is involved either: corrected 2026-08-22, out of the first sanity sweep's verification round.** This entry used to assert the password cost outright — *"clearing it costs them their login password"* — with the *"no first-party page read says it does"* attaching only to the re-trigger; item 5 now carries the Apple page that fails to support it, and the measurement taken beside it. The architecture keeps the installed path and replaces the complete verified bundle there ([20.2](#202-macos-replace-the-complete-signed-bundle) Z8); item 5 states what breaks. **If the fact closes the other way, it is a change to what the user gets and returns to Specs then.**

**The Windows twin of that clause, which this document exposed.** Whether the reputation warning fires on a build the application launches itself. Same verdict, same reasoning, and one difference recorded rather than smoothed: **the Windows shape cannot take the macOS precaution**, because side-by-side puts the new build at a new path by construction (Z17).

**The Start-menu shortcut Windows notifications require.** The user gets an entry in their own Start menu that no spec mentions. **Declined:** it is ordinary, per-user, needs no elevation, and **without it Row 284 cannot be kept on Windows at all**. The former installer question is closed: Microsoft's worked route has the ordinary application create and install the per-user shortcut itself (E3). The Windows cost never becomes an installer.

**The macOS notification permission prompt.** The user meets a system prompt the first time Smoozy would post. **Declined:** Apple's model is that the app **must** request authorization, so there is no route to the promise without it; the prompt is attached to the first real event rather than to application start (T7), which is the least the platform permits; and what the user meets at that moment is already Design's under that concern's own marker.

**A Linux computer with no notification service.** The user gets nothing where the four kinds would have arrived. **Declined:** it is an absence rather than a place to look, something to type or a thing that can be lost; every one of the four also leaves its own durable in-app record; and the specification itself says a client *"must not assume"* the service is available, so this is the platform's own case and not a cost the architecture introduces.

**The copy of the local record taken before a migration** ([5.2](#52-migrations) L8). It spends a little of the user's disk without asking. **Declined:** it is not a place to look, something to type, or a thing that can be lost, and **the opposite ruling would have been the escalation** — a rollback that met an unreadable record would cost the user their history, their servers and their settings, which is precisely a thing that can be lost.

**The previous Windows build kept until the new one has started** ([20.4](#204-windows-side-by-side-and-never-in-place) Z15). One extra copy on disk, removed after a successful start. **Declined:** same reasoning, and side-by-side gives no other way to recover a failed update.

**The macOS keychain dialog after an update.** **Declined, and it is not this document's ruling to make:** `Security and licence` §3.1 already takes the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-16 on what the keychain costs the user, and hands this document only the requirement to treat the dialog as an ordinary outcome of its own updater rather than as a failure path. [9](#9-the-secret-adapter-and-its-helper) S9 does exactly that and adds nothing.

**A supported system whose web view is absent or too old.** H13 weighs four documented options and **takes option *Embed* on the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-23**, so on a Windows machine with no suitable runtime the application offers to run the embedded bootstrapper rather than saying it cannot run. *(This said H13 takes option **Error**, so the application says so rather than running — which was a downloaded binary that would not start, on a system Row 282 says Smoozy runs on; and before that it said H13 declines **both** documented options, corrected 2026-08-22 with H13 itself, which carries four. The ruling is the paragraph at the head of this section and the history is kept because it is what the escalation was made of.)* **It reaches the escalation, and the ground it was declined on is gone — corrected 2026-08-22, out of the first sanity sweep's verification round.** This entry used to read *"It does not reach the escalation today, and the reason is the one item 5 already gives: the cost is not established rather than small"*, resting on *"No reading says any supported configuration lacks a current-enough web view, and there is no computer to pose it on for two of the three, so an escalation put on an unestablished cost would ask the Creator to rule on a hypothesis."* **Both halves are answered by reading and neither needed a computer**: Microsoft's distribution page says *"some devices might not have the Runtime pre-installed"* and Canonical's Ubuntu 24.04.3 server manifest carries no `libwebkit*` and no `libgtk-3-0` at all (H16); and Wails prices the alternative — option Embed, *"~150k to the binary size"* (H13). **So the escalation was put on a figure rather than a hypothesis, and it was filed on 2026-08-23 and ruled the same day** — this entry read *"and it has not been filed: filing it is owed"* until then. [Section 22](#22-unresolved) item 23 stated what breaks in the words the escalation used and is closed on the ruling; **what the ruling does not reach is items 48 and 49**, and each says what breaks in its own words.

**The zero-byte reservation standing at the final name, and this is the one that reached the Creator in Round 1** — the web-view answer above reached them later, on 2026-08-23. C13 identified the thing itself — *"it is a file the user could meet"* — and this section had not asked its own question of it. The file sits in the folder [Section 8](#8-where-a-download-is-proposed-to-land) proposed to the user, carries the exact name a finished frame carries, holds nothing, and is left behind where Smoozy is stopped inside the window, cleared only at the next run. **That is a place to look and a thing that can be lost, so it reached the escalation and was put to the Creator by the runner in Round 1.** **The Creator ruled on 2026-08-18 that it is acceptable.** So **C10 stands exactly as written**, `Download` carries the user-visible consequence in the spec, and [Section 22](#22-unresolved) item 30 carries the window and what it costs. **What the ruling turned on is recorded rather than smoothed:** the two alternatives are the two this document already names as traps — judging takenness by comparing strings, which C11 calls modelling and which is how seventeen frames became fourteen files on 2026-08-17, or releasing the name before the rename, which reopens the window C10 exists to close.

**The two faces, added 2026-08-21** ([2.7](#27-the-two-faces) H30). **Declined.** Nothing is added to a screen, nothing is asked, and the worst outcome the answer permits is text in a face that is not the platform's own — which is a look rather than a cost, and the column the promise is actually about goes on lining up by the standard's own guarantee.

**The colour scheme, both rows, added 2026-08-21** ([2.6](#26-the-colour-scheme-read-and-declared) H24). **Declined, and it is the clearest decline in the list**: the answer puts nothing on a screen, adds nothing to Settings, asks for nothing to be typed and creates nothing that can be lost. The user meets the theme their operating system is already in, which is Design's promise word for word. **The one case worth naming is a Linux desktop with no colour-scheme setting at all**, where Smoozy draws light and says nothing — and that is an absence rather than a cost, on the same reasoning as the Linux computer with no notification service above.
---

## 24. What this document does not own

Cited, and deliberately not restated or decided here.

- **The store inventory of the local record, the identities, desired-versus-observed state, the event journal and acknowledgement, reconciliation on both sides, the failure record's shape and its condition table, the repeat key itself, the atomic-or-partial boundary, the deletion tombstones, and every local lifetime** — `State, identity and recovery`, Rows 285 and 292.
- **Request framing and versioning, capability negotiation, the authentication boundary, correlation and idempotency, per-waiter deadlines, the transfer staging-and-publication contract, per-channel rate enforcement, the free-space reading and the disk-full condition's two observed values, and the reporting floor** — `Server protocol and transfers`, Rows 281, 287, 289, 291 and 306.
- **The secret store on each system, the local record's protection and its non-encryption, the crossing lists, host trust, the erasure order and its recorded debt, logging redaction** — `Security and licence`, Row 278.
- **The package transaction, boot registration, the machine-local endpoint, worker lifecycles and termination contracts, the captured streams and the explicit collection outcome, the machine-held answer-release path, the arbitration mechanism, health readings, and what a machine does in response to a repair request** — `Rented-server runtime`, Row 286.
- **The server directory layout, the submission manifest, output redirect, the expected-output inventory, the completeness statement, where derivatives are made, and when a machine-side derivative is cleared** — `Job files and outputs`, Rows 118 to 139 and 297.
- **When a connection is made or replaced, the connected period, reconnect policy, and the connection condition's own observed value** — `Servers`, Rows 24 to 46.
- **Destination rules, format narrowing, expected counts, finished-file detection, continuation, and the Replace and Keep response built on this document's collision judgement** — `Download`, Rows 202 to 220, 298 and 299.
- **Whether a system file picker can take files and folders together, remote listing, permission handling, and stale listings** — `File Browser`, Rows 180 to 201, 307, 340 to 346, 428 and 486. *(Corrected 2026-08-29 at the stage-close repair: the list read **Rows 180 to 201 and 307** and undercounted that document's set by nine — the nine added are its Design rows, 340 to 346, 428 and 486.)*
- **What composes the one queue, the catch-up notice's own content, held jobs and groups, idle timing, adoption, and what a history entry shows** — `Queue & History`, Rows 47 to 87 and 302.
- **The user-facing fallback re-run for supervisor-independent package repair, and the restart a setup step needs** — `Server Setup`, Rows 88 to 117, 283, 295 and 308–309.
- **What each notification says, what clicking one does, how the per-kind switches are offered, where the restore control sits, the clearing gesture, where a speed reading sits, what unit a cap is shown in, and — added 2026-08-21 — which of *Rendering* and *Reconnecting…* occupies the work column's one slot when both are true** — `Design`. **Two of those carry a bound from this document rather than a free hand**, both added 2026-08-21: what a per-kind switch may claim about the picture ([T17a](#134-the-frame-preview-and-it-is-three-answers), Row 332), and that a speed reading is one per direction over one fixed window and is held rather than aged between publications ([R15a](#114-the-achieved-rate-reading), Row 329). **Four more joined on 2026-08-25, each Design's with a bound**: the pane seams' drawn narrowing steps, which write nothing ([3.3](#33-ui-event-delivery) P11); the About readings' wording, which says a moment and never an age ([20.1](#201-what-the-application-does-and-what-the-user-does) Z23a); the Linux update box's wording, offered once per version (Z10b); and the accelerator labels beside the menu items, drawn in each system's own notation with no combination invented where a platform publishes none ([18.5](#185-the-keyboard-shortcuts-beside-the-menu-items) K15). **Two more joined on 2026-08-29, each Design's with a bound**: the words beside a failure that said nothing about why, bounded by **no line of the ordinary output lifted out of it and nothing composed from it put where the reason is said** ([16.3](#163-row-286-presented-the-plain-sentence-and-the-machines-own-words) M13b, Row 504); and where the user's own *check for updates* act sits and what it reads while it runs, bounded by **one check and one mechanism, a wait on admission that is *underway* and not a fifth reading, and the Linux box offered on that route for a version the declined mark names** ([20.1](#201-what-the-application-does-and-what-the-user-does) Z26–Z30, Row 508). **One more joined later on 2026-08-29 at the stage-close repair, Design's with a bound**: the wording and placement of the telling that names what removing an upload request record costs, `File Browser` N8's requirement taken into [Section 21](#21-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document) — bounded by **not a dismissible warning and no suppression row, not a failure record, and not a box**, `Reporting a problem` C2 admitting exactly one interruption outside the failure shape and forbidding a second to cite it. **Which menu carries *Report a problem…* was with the Creator and is ruled**: the Creator ruled for the spec on 2026-08-25, the item sits in the Smoozy menu, **Row 481 is struck**, the Help menu it was drawn in is gone, and K5 and K6 were rewritten to four menus the same day. *(Corrected 2026-08-29 at the second stage-close re-sweep. This sentence had gone on saying the question was open and that K6 stood until it was ruled, four days after both halves were falsified by K5 in this same document — and a re-sweep that reads K5 and K6 and stops would never meet it.)*
